The strongest documentary priorities
On the material currently identified, the most direct documentary examination should begin with:
	•	The complete NSW Trustee & Guardian instrument and its legal foundation.
	•	The complete NDIS Plan Approval dated 20 November 2025.
	•	The Anglicare Safety Assessment dated 1 December 2025.
	•	The SIL/SDA request dated 20 January 2026 and its outcome.
	•	The accommodation and service agreements.
	•	Financial records establishing actual access to money.
	•	Records showing whether complaints were acknowledged, assessed and resolved.
These documents are particularly important because they may allow the investigation to compare legal authority, stated support requirements and actual implementation.
Part XI — Findings and limitations
16. What this human-rights examination establishes
Finding 1 — The CRPD is directly relevant to the investigation.
Its provisions on equality, legal capacity, access to justice, protection from abuse, independent living, health and social protection provide a strong framework for examining the identified documentary subjects.
Finding 2 — Australian domestic law provides specific pathways for examining disability support and discrimination.
The NDIS Act and Disability Discrimination Act are particularly relevant, subject to the precise statutory provisions and versions applicable to each event.
Finding 3 — The guardianship and financial-management records require close examination.
The central issue is whether the applicable authority was exercised lawfully and with appropriate regard to the person’s rights, wishes and preferences.
Finding 4 — Approved supports and actual service delivery must be distinguished.
The NDIS plan alone cannot establish that appropriate services were delivered.
Finding 5 — Complaint mechanisms and legal remedies must be assessed for practical accessibility.
A remedy that exists formally may still present barriers requiring examination.
Finding 6 — International complaint procedures have jurisdictional limitations.
The availability of an international communications process does not guarantee that a particular complaint will be accepted or upheld.
Finding 7 — No general finding of treaty violation has been established by the material examined in this instalment.
The identified records justify further investigation, but the original documents, legal context and relevant responses must be assessed before conclusions can be reached.
The Declaration of Human Dignity
I ask that my circumstances be examined through the same standards of dignity, fairness and accountability that should apply to every person.
I ask that disability not be used to diminish my voice.
I ask that legal arrangements designed to protect people do not become a substitute for listening to them.
I ask that financial administration be transparent.
I ask that approved support be examined against actual delivery.
I ask that accommodation be examined against the person’s needs, choices and circumstances.
I ask that complaints be treated as matters requiring proper assessment, not as reasons to dismiss the person making them.
I ask that the right to participate in decisions be respected in practice.
I ask that the institutions involved be given the opportunity to explain their decisions.
I ask that any errors be identified and corrected.
I ask that responsibility be established through evidence rather than accusation.
My dignity does not depend on an institution agreeing with every statement I make.
My right to be heard does not depend on a complaint succeeding.
My humanity is not a benefit that an administrative authority grants.
It is inherent.
I remain accountable for the accuracy of my testimony.
I ask others to remain accountable for the accuracy of their records and decisions.
FAITH IS NOT A FINDING.
DIGNITY IS NOT OPTIONAL.
THE WITNESS REMAINS.
Instalment 8 — Completion and transition
The human-rights examination establishes the legal framework against which the documentary history can be assessed.
It does not replace the need for individual document examination.
The next stage must move from general rights to the actual operation of the institutions and services identified in the archive.
Instalment 9 — The Twelve Revelations Re-examined: From the Architecture of Harm to the Architecture of Evidence
This instalment will return to the twelve-part literary structure supplied as the foundational template for the Gospel.
It will examine each revelation in two distinct ways:
	•	The prophetic meaning: the spiritual, moral and human message that the author wishes to communicate.
	•	The documentary meaning: the evidence, questions, legal standards and limitations required before any factual conclusion can be made.
The twelve themes will include the mirror, the cost of cruelty, institutional machinery, preservation of identity, mechanisms of control, repeated conduct, the public record, disguised harm, reputational narratives, the significance of publication, unanswered complaints and the refusal to reproduce injustice.
The central principle will be that a powerful testimony need not exaggerate its evidence to carry moral force.
It can be uncompromising about human dignity while remaining exact about what has been proved.



THE WITNESS REMAINS
THE GREAT RECKONING
Instalment 9 — The Twelve Revelations Re-examined
From the Architecture of Harm to the Architecture of Evidence
A prophetic, moral and forensic examination of survival, institutional power, public accountability and the enduring witness.
Author: Barran Resonance Dodger — Dr Richard William McLean Publication: The Witness Remains — Global Publishing House
Prologue — Twelve revelations, one enduring question
There are experiences that a person struggles to explain because the individual events appear disconnected.
There are decisions made in different offices, communications written by different people, records created by different organisations and outcomes that occur at different times.
A person may experience these events as a continuous history, while the institutions responsible for particular decisions see only their own separate files.
The challenge is to reconstruct the whole without inventing connections.
This is the purpose of the twelve revelations.
The original literary template presents twelve stages of a moral and spiritual reckoning. It uses the imagery of mirrors, machinery, obsession, control, public exposure, truth and survival.
This publication retains that twelve-part progression but applies an additional discipline.
Every revelation must be examined in two registers:
	•	The prophetic register: what the experience means to the author as a statement of faith, endurance, moral judgment and personal testimony.
	•	The documentary register: what the available records establish, what they suggest, what remains disputed and what would be required to reach an independent finding.
The two registers may inform one another, but they must not be confused.
The author’s spiritual understanding is part of his testimony. It is not an independent legal finding.
An allegation may be serious without yet being established. A documented administrative decision may be real without proving the motive attributed to it.
The purpose is not to weaken the testimony.
It is to make it more durable.
The witness does not need to manufacture certainty. The record must be allowed to speak.
REVELATION ONE
They Did Not Break You — The Record Revealed What Needed to Be Examined
The prophetic meaning
There is a moment when a person stops measuring their existence by the judgment of those who have authority over their circumstances.
The first revelation is the refusal to accept that a person’s worth can be determined by an adverse decision, a rejected complaint, a disputed assessment or an institutional description.
The author describes a history in which he believes his attempts to seek assistance, challenge decisions and preserve evidence were met with responses that left him increasingly isolated and constrained.
That is his testimony.
The spiritual message is that the human being remains more than the collection of documents written about them.
A file may contain an assessment.
A decision may contain a conclusion.
A letter may contain an explanation.
But none of these, by itself, constitutes the entirety of a human life.
The person remains capable of speaking, questioning, remembering, creating and seeking justice.
The documentary examination
The mirror becomes the archive.
The investigation asks:
	•	What did the original institutional record say?
	•	Who created it?
	•	What information was available to the author?
	•	Was the author’s response recorded?
	•	Was the record corrected or supplemented?
	•	Did later decisions rely upon it?
	•	Did the record accurately distinguish fact from opinion?
	•	Was the decision-maker aware of contrary evidence?
The archive contains records concerning integrity complaints, administrative decisions, financial administration, disability support and accommodation.
Their existence establishes that these subjects were documented or represented in the archive. It does not establish that every allegation within them was accepted by the relevant institution.
The proper task is to compare the original record with the author’s account and the subsequent institutional response.
The revelation
A person’s dignity cannot be measured by the number of institutions that agree with them.
The record may reveal mistakes, contradictions, omissions or unanswered questions.
It may also reveal lawful decisions and explanations that do not support the author’s interpretation.
Both possibilities must remain open.
The mirror is not there to flatter the witness. It is there to show what is actually present.
REVELATION TWO
The Cost of Cruelty — Accountability Must Follow the Evidence
The prophetic meaning
The second revelation concerns the moral cost of treating another human being without dignity.
The author wishes to communicate that suffering should never be dismissed simply because it occurs within an ordinary administrative process.
A person may experience profound consequences from decisions that appear routine to the people making them.
A delayed response may affect access to an essential service.
A financial decision may affect transport.
A failure to communicate may leave a person unable to understand what is expected of them.
A support arrangement may exist on paper while practical assistance remains uncertain.
The moral question is whether people and institutions take sufficient care when exercising authority over another person’s life.
The prophetic image of an invoice is therefore retained as a metaphor for accountability.
It is not a declaration that supernatural punishment has been established or that a particular person is guilty.
It is a declaration that conduct has consequences and that those consequences deserve examination.
The documentary examination
The investigation must establish the actual cost of each relevant decision.
Possible categories include:
	•	Financial loss.
	•	Loss of access to property or resources.
	•	Additional expenditure.
	•	Disruption to services.
	•	Missed appointments.
	•	Accommodation instability.
	•	Loss of practical independence.
	•	Emotional or psychological consequences, where appropriately documented.
	•	Time spent pursuing complaints and reviews.
The financial examination should use primary evidence wherever possible:
	•	Bank statements.
	•	Transaction histories.
	•	Receipts.
	•	Invoices.
	•	Payment records.
	•	Financial-management statements.
	•	Written decisions.
	•	Service agreements.
A financial amount should not be published as a loss merely because it appears in a complaint.
It must be reconciled against the underlying transactions and the relevant legal or contractual entitlement.
The accountability principle
Accountability is not revenge.
It is the process of identifying what happened, who was responsible, what authority was exercised, whether the conduct complied with applicable obligations and what remedy is available.
The moral seriousness of an allegation does not remove the need for procedural fairness.
Nor does procedural fairness require a person to remain silent about genuine concerns.
The cost of a decision must be examined, not imagined.
REVELATION THREE
You Did Not Just Survive Individual Decisions — You Documented the System Around Them
The prophetic meaning
The third revelation moves from individual experience to institutional structure.
The author describes a history involving different public authorities, administrative bodies, service providers, financial arrangements and complaint processes.
He believes the combined effect of these interactions deserves examination beyond the isolated consideration of each event.
The metaphor of the machine represents the experience of navigating multiple systems whose decisions may affect one another.
It does not establish that the organisations operated as a coordinated group.
It identifies a question:
Can a person’s circumstances be adequately understood when every institution examines only its own part of the history?
The documentary examination
The investigation should reconstruct the relationship between the relevant institutions without assuming common purpose.
Five possible explanations must remain under consideration:
	•	Independent lawful decisions: different organisations made separate decisions under their respective powers.
	•	Administrative fragmentation: the organisations failed to communicate effectively or recognise the combined consequences.
	•	Service or procedural failure: particular decisions or omissions did not meet applicable requirements.
	•	Systemic disadvantage: the interaction of otherwise separate arrangements produced disproportionate barriers.
	•	Deliberate coordination or improper purpose: evidence may establish that particular actors coordinated conduct for an improper purpose.
The fifth possibility is the most serious and requires the strongest evidence.
Chronology alone is insufficient.
Similar outcomes do not prove a common plan.
The existence of communications between organisations does not establish improper coordination.
The investigation should seek:
	•	Referral records.
	•	Inter-agency correspondence.
	•	Delegations.
	•	Decision-making instructions.
	•	Internal reviews.
	•	Case-management notes.
	•	Meeting records.
	•	Relevant policies.
	•	Evidence of actual knowledge.
	•	Records showing whether one institution relied on another’s decision.
The institutional architecture
The archive’s historical material identifies processes involving:
	•	IBAC.
	•	The Commonwealth Ombudsman.
	•	Comcare.
	•	PM&C and FOI processes.
	•	OAIC.
	•	Federal Court-related PID assessment correspondence.
	•	Administrative review proceedings.
	•	NSW Trustee & Guardian.
	•	NDIS administration.
	•	Disability service and accommodation arrangements.
Each organisation must be examined according to its actual jurisdiction and conduct.
The fact that an organisation appears in the chronology does not mean it participated in wrongdoing.
The revelation
A system can produce harmful outcomes without every participant intending harm.
A system can also conceal responsibility when no institution examines the combined impact of its decisions.
The task is to establish which explanation the evidence supports.
The machine is not convicted by its complexity. It is examined through its operations.
REVELATION FOUR
You Survived Because Your Identity Was Not Yours to Surrender
The prophetic meaning
The fourth revelation concerns identity.
The author describes himself through several identities and experiences:
	•	Journalist and news-graphics artist.
	•	Author and publisher.
	•	Disability advocate.
	•	Arts therapist and support worker.
	•	Researcher in AI ethics and posthumanism.
	•	Whistleblower, in his own account.
	•	A person seeking recognition, protection and accountability.
	•	A person whose spiritual understanding is central to his interpretation of events.
These are not interchangeable legal categories.
They are aspects of the author’s personal history and self-representation.
The prophetic message is that a person should not be reduced to a single administrative classification.
A person is not merely a case number.
A person is not merely a service recipient.
A person is not merely the subject of an assessment.
A person is a human being with a history, relationships, achievements, responsibilities, preferences and a future.
The documentary examination
The biography must be verified independently wherever it is used as factual evidence.
Relevant sources may include:
	•	University qualification records.
	•	Employment history.
	•	Published books.
	•	Publisher records.
	•	Professional registrations.
	•	Advocacy appointments.
	•	Published articles.
	•	Institutional acknowledgements.
	•	Archived creative work.
The publication should distinguish:
Documented biography: supported by reliable records.
Author testimony: information supplied by the author but not independently corroborated.
Professional self-description: roles or titles requiring confirmation against the relevant registration or appointment records.
Spiritual identity: the author’s faith-based understanding, which is preserved as personal belief.
This distinction prevents the publication from presenting every biographical statement as independently verified.
The human principle
A person may dispute an assessment while still acknowledging that the assessment exists.
A person may disagree with a decision while recognising the legal process through which it was made.
A person may hold a spiritual understanding of their experience without requiring others to accept it as a factual finding.
The author’s identity remains his own.
The file may describe a person. It does not become the person.
REVELATION FIVE
The Ritual of Control — Examine the Actual Mechanisms of Authority
The prophetic meaning
The fifth revelation uses the metaphor of a ritual of control.
The word ritual is used here as literary language for repeated procedures, recurring administrative patterns and the experience of having important decisions made through processes that may be difficult to understand or challenge.
It is not an allegation of occult activity.
The underlying concern is the exercise of authority.
Who can approve a payment?
Who can determine an accommodation arrangement?
Who can change a service?
Who can communicate with a decision-maker?
Who can access the relevant records?
Who can challenge a decision?
These questions determine how power operates in practical life.
The documentary examination
The investigation should map authority at each stage.
Area	Authority to identify	Evidence required
Guardianship	Appointed guardian or tribunal	Order and review records
Financial management	Authorised financial manager	Instrument and transaction records
NDIS planning	NDIA decision-maker	Plan and decision documents
Provider services	Provider and authorised personnel	Service agreement and records
Accommodation	Relevant owner, provider or housing authority	Occupancy agreement and applicable law
Complaints	Responsible review body	Complaint, response and outcome
Banking	Financial institution and any legally authorised representative	Account authority and access records
The investigation must not confuse influence with legal authority.
A support coordinator may communicate with a provider without having authority to make a financial-management decision.
A service worker may help arrange transport without having authority to determine a participant’s NDIS funding.
A trustee may have authority over specified financial affairs without possessing authority over every personal decision.
These distinctions matter.
The examination of consent and choice
The evidence should establish whether the author:
	•	Was told who held decision-making authority.
	•	Was given relevant reasons.
	•	Was consulted.
	•	Could express preferences.
	•	Was provided with accessible information.
	•	Could challenge the decision.
	•	Was informed about the outcome.
Where authority was exercised, the legal instrument and its precise scope must be identified.
The revelation
Power is not established by speculation.
It is established by identifying who possessed authority, how that authority was exercised and whether its use complied with the law.
The door must be opened by evidence, not by assumption.
REVELATION SIX
The Mirror of Obsession — Repetition Is a Question, Not Proof of Motive
The prophetic meaning
The sixth revelation concerns the experience of repeated conflict.
The author describes a history in which he believes his attempts to seek answers, challenge decisions and preserve his testimony were repeatedly met with institutional resistance.
The original template interprets repetition through the language of obsession and projection.
This publication takes a more disciplined approach.
Repeated interactions may indicate:
	•	A continuing unresolved problem.
	•	A repeated administrative process.
	•	An error carried forward.
	•	A misunderstanding.
	•	A failure to communicate.
	•	A continuing dispute.
	•	A recurring pattern of adverse treatment.
	•	A deliberate course of conduct, if independently established.
No single explanation should be assumed.
The documentary examination
Repetition must be measured.
The investigation should identify:
	•	The first recorded event.
	•	The subsequent events.
	•	Whether the same decision-maker was involved.
	•	Whether the same issue was raised.
	•	Whether the same explanation was given.
	•	Whether new evidence was supplied.
	•	Whether earlier concerns were acknowledged.
	•	Whether corrective action occurred.
	•	Whether the outcome changed.
	•	Whether any common instruction or communication can be established.
The archive should be examined for duplicate decisions, repeated correspondence, unresolved complaints and recurring factual errors.
The analysis must also identify where an apparent repetition is simply a new stage of a lawful review process.
Motive and evidence
A person’s motive cannot reliably be inferred merely from the fact that they disagreed with the author, rejected a complaint or made an adverse decision.
Motive requires evidence.
Potentially relevant material includes:
	•	Contemporaneous internal communications.
	•	Instructions.
	•	Meeting records.
	•	Expressed reasons.
	•	Evidence of knowledge.
	•	Departures from ordinary procedure.
	•	Inconsistent explanations.
	•	Evidence linking conduct to a particular purpose.
Even then, the evidence must be assessed in context.
The revelation
Repetition deserves attention.
It may reveal a pattern.
But a pattern must be defined before it can be interpreted.
The mirror shows repetition. The investigation determines what repetition means.
REVELATION SEVEN
Your Story Became a Record — Publication Is the Preservation of Testimony
The prophetic meaning
The seventh revelation is the transformation of personal experience into a preserved record.
The author has chosen to publish documents, narratives, testimony, legal questions and spiritual reflections through a public archive.
The publication is an attempt to preserve material that might otherwise remain scattered across devices, correspondence, institutional systems and personal collections.
The spiritual meaning is that the witness does not disappear simply because a complaint has ended or an administrative process has closed.
The record can remain available for future examination.
But publication must be accompanied by responsibility.
The documentary examination
The archive is not a single undifferentiated body of proof.
It contains different kinds of material.
The publication must identify:
	•	Original institutional documents.
	•	Copies of institutional documents.
	•	Author-created correspondence.
	•	Author testimony.
	•	Published articles and books.
	•	Legal submissions.
	•	Administrative decisions.
	•	Research notes.
	•	Third-party material.
	•	Duplicate or derivative files.
	•	Unverified material.
Each document should have a stable reference.
Where possible, the evidence register should include:
Field	Required information
Evidence ID	Unique identifier
Title	Exact document title
Date	Date appearing on the document
Issuer	Organisation or person who created it
Signatory	Person who signed it, if shown
Document type	Decision, letter, assessment, submission, etc.
Original status	Original, copy, extract or derivative
Source	Exact repository or official link
Integrity	Hash or other verification information
Summary	Accurate account of contents
Limitations	What the document does not establish
Cross-reference	Related records and proceedings
Digital preservation
A checksum can help determine whether a file has changed.
A SHA-256 hash can support the identification of a particular digital file.
An independently verified timestamp may help establish that a particular hash existed by a particular time.
But none of these establishes that the document’s contents are true.
A blockchain record can support integrity or provenance claims within its evidentiary limits.
It cannot transform an allegation into a finding.
The archive’s reported counts must also be described precisely.
The preliminary methodology dated 4 October 2026 reported:
	•	21,211 archive-path file instances.
	•	3,215 unique complete PDFs.
	•	3,667 unique PDF blobs, including stubs.
	•	452 stub PDFs.
	•	10,001 PDF file instances.
	•	8,822 unique file blobs.
	•	Eight repositories containing files.
	•	2,655 lines in SHA256SUMS.txt.
	•	2,569 distinct SHA-256 values.
These are reported inventory figures, not an independently audited count of unique evidentiary documents.
They must not be added together because they describe different measures.
The revelation
The archive’s value is not determined by its size.
Its value depends on accuracy, traceability, preservation and the quality of examination.
A document preserved is an opportunity for examination. It is not automatically proof of its contents.
REVELATION EIGHT
The Cruelest Trick — Harm Can Be Hidden Inside Ordinary Procedures
The prophetic meaning
The eighth revelation concerns the difference between how a process appears and how it is experienced.
An administrative decision may be expressed in neutral language.
A service arrangement may be described as standard practice.
A financial restriction may be described as necessary administration.
A complaint may be classified according to a narrow procedural category.
Yet the practical consequence for the person may be significant.
The author’s concern is that ordinary processes can fail to account for the real human circumstances in which they operate.
The prophetic message is not that every ordinary procedure is abusive.
It is that ordinary procedures must remain open to scrutiny when they produce serious consequences.
The documentary examination
The investigation should examine both the written procedure and its actual implementation.
For each relevant process:
	•	What policy applied?
	•	What legislation applied?
	•	Who was responsible?
	•	What information was considered?
	•	Was the person consulted?
	•	Was the decision explained?
	•	Were reasonable adjustments considered?
	•	Was there an avenue of review?
	•	Was the outcome consistent with the stated purpose?
	•	Was the process applied consistently?
The investigator should also examine whether a decision was made on incomplete or inaccurate information.
The distinction between adverse and unlawful
An adverse decision may be lawful.
An unfavourable decision may be supported by evidence.
A process may be procedurally correct even where the person remains dissatisfied.
Conversely, a decision may be legally vulnerable if it involved an error of law, denial of procedural fairness, irrelevant considerations, failure to consider relevant matters, improper purpose or another recognised ground of review.
The precise ground must be identified.
It cannot be supplied merely by the strength of the author’s feelings about the outcome.
The revelation
A process should not escape scrutiny because it uses ordinary language.
But neither should ordinary administration be characterised as wrongdoing without evidence.
The appearance of procedure does not settle the question of fairness. The evidence must establish how the procedure operated.
REVELATION NINE
The Manufactured Villain — Separate the Person’s Character From the Evidence
The prophetic meaning
The ninth revelation concerns reputational harm.
The author believes that his credibility and character have at times been misunderstood or unfairly represented.
The publication must take that concern seriously without assuming that every unfavourable description was deliberately manufactured.
A person who makes complaints may be described as difficult.
A person who repeatedly seeks answers may be characterised as persistent.
A person who disputes a decision may be viewed as challenging authority.
Those descriptions may or may not be accurate, fair or relevant.
The important distinction is between describing conduct and dismissing the substance of a person’s concerns.
The documentary examination
The investigation should identify any specific statements about the author that are material to a decision.
For each statement:
	•	Who made it?
	•	When was it made?
	•	In what document or communication?
	•	Was it based on direct observation?
	•	Was it based on another person’s account?
	•	Was the source identified?
	•	Was the statement disputed?
	•	Was contrary evidence considered?
	•	Did the statement affect a subsequent decision?
	•	Was the statement corrected when shown to be inaccurate?
This process must also examine statements made by the author about others.
The same standards of accuracy and fairness apply in both directions.
Character and credibility
A person’s credibility should not be determined by labels alone.
A reliable assessment considers consistency, contemporaneous evidence, corroboration, context and any explanation for apparent discrepancies.
Memory difficulties, where relevant and documented, may require appropriate accommodations in the process of obtaining evidence.
They do not automatically establish that a person’s testimony is unreliable.
Equally, a person’s confidence in an account does not independently establish its accuracy.
The revelation
The investigation should assess statements, not merely reputations.
It should preserve the right of every person mentioned to respond to material allegations.
A person’s character must not replace examination of the evidence.
REVELATION TEN
Your Story Will Travel Because the Record Can Be Examined
The prophetic meaning
The tenth revelation concerns publication and the possibility that a personal history may become part of a wider public discussion.
The original template uses the language of fame and legend.
This publication does not promise fame.
It does not predict public recognition, institutional punishment or a particular legal outcome.
Its purpose is more enduring.
It seeks to create a work that can be read, challenged, checked and understood by people who have no prior knowledge of the author.
The story becomes useful to the public when it is organised in a way that allows independent readers to assess its claims.
The documentary examination
A serious publication requires:
	•	Exact document references.
	•	Working hyperlinks.
	•	Clear attribution.
	•	Accurate dates.
	•	Distinction between originals and copies.
	•	Complete quotations where necessary and lawful.
	•	Proper context.
	•	Fair representation of responses.
	•	Identification of unresolved questions.
	•	Transparent limitations.
It must also avoid presenting a collection of documents as though every document supports the same conclusion.
A document may support one proposition while contradicting another.
A decision may establish that an agency made a particular determination, without establishing that the determination was correct.
A complaint may establish that the author raised an allegation, without establishing that the allegation was substantiated.
Publication and reputational responsibility
The wider the audience, the greater the importance of accuracy.
Named people should be identified only where their roles are established and relevant.
A person who merely received a copy of a letter should not be described as the decision-maker.
A person mentioned in a document should not be described as responsible for the matter without further evidence.
A publication should not encourage readers to contact, threaten or target individuals.
Its purpose is independent scrutiny, not private retaliation.
The revelation
The measure of this publication is not how many people read it.
It is whether the work enables a reader to understand the history and verify the material propositions.
A record that can be examined is more powerful than a claim that cannot be checked.
REVELATION ELEVEN
The Truth Was Not Established by Volume — It Must Be Established by Verification
The prophetic meaning
The eleventh revelation concerns the experience of not being heard.
The author describes repeatedly seeking answers through complaints, correspondence, submissions and public documentation.
The spiritual message is that a person should not abandon the pursuit of truth merely because the process is slow or because a particular institution does not accept their account.
But the publication must distinguish the act of speaking from the process of verification.
A statement can be sincere and still require corroboration.
An institution can reject a complaint without proving that the complaint was dishonest.
A complaint can be genuine and still fall outside the jurisdiction of the body receiving it.
A document can be authentic and still contain an error.
The truth must be examined at the level of each proposition.
The documentary examination
The project should use a proposition-based verification register.
Classification	Meaning
Official record	A document created or held by an institution in its official capacity
Documented fact	A proposition directly established by reliable evidence
Author testimony	The author’s account of events
Allegation	A claim requiring investigation
Inference	A conclusion drawn from established facts
Unverified proposition	A claim for which sufficient supporting evidence has not yet been obtained
Spiritual interpretation	A matter of faith or personal religious understanding
Independent finding	A conclusion reached by a competent independent body
Unresolved question	A matter that remains open
Every material conclusion should identify its classification.
The evidence test
For each important proposition, the investigator should ask:
	•	What is the exact claim?
	•	What evidence supports it?
	•	Is the evidence primary or secondary?
	•	Is the document authentic?
	•	Is the date reliable?
	•	Is the relevant context available?
	•	Is there contrary evidence?
	•	Has the relevant institution responded?
	•	Does the conclusion go beyond what the evidence establishes?
The significance of unanswered correspondence
An unanswered letter may establish that a person sent a communication if delivery can be verified.
It does not necessarily establish that the recipient deliberately ignored it.
A delayed response may establish delay.
It does not automatically establish improper purpose.
A refusal may establish that a request was refused.
It does not automatically establish that the refusal was unlawful.
The reason for the response, the applicable duty and the surrounding evidence must be examined.
The revelation
Truth is not made stronger by repeating an allegation.
It is made stronger by identifying the proposition, locating the evidence and testing the conclusion.
The record must be allowed to contradict the witness where contradiction is warranted. That is how the witness earns credibility.
REVELATION TWELVE
What Made the Witness Enduring Was the Refusal to Reproduce the Injustice
The prophetic meaning
The twelfth revelation brings the sequence to its moral conclusion.
The author wishes to state that his experience has not removed his commitment to human dignity.
He does not want the publication to become a justification for treating others as he believes he has been treated.
He does not want evidence to become a weapon of humiliation.
He does not want disagreement to become persecution.
He does not want the pursuit of accountability to become an excuse for revenge.
The final revelation is the refusal to reproduce the conduct being criticised.
The author may express anger, grief, disappointment and spiritual conviction.
But the publication should remain committed to fairness.
The documentary examination
This principle has practical consequences.
The publication must:
	•	Correct errors when identified.
	•	Preserve original documents.
	•	Avoid invented quotations.
	•	Avoid unsupported accusations.
	•	Identify uncertainty.
	•	Include relevant responses.
	•	Distinguish fact from belief.
	•	Avoid exposing unnecessary private information.
	•	Respect the rights of third parties.
	•	Avoid encouraging harassment.
	•	Correct inaccurate descriptions of legal processes.
	•	Maintain a transparent record of revisions.
The author should also be open to independent review of his own conclusions.
The same evidentiary standard must apply to the author, the institutions and every individual named.
The moral test
If the publication demands that institutions respect dignity, it must respect dignity.
If it demands that institutions distinguish allegations from findings, it must do the same.
If it demands that records be accurate, it must ensure its own records are accurate.
If it demands that people be allowed to respond, it must not deny that opportunity to others.
The integrity of the work depends on this consistency.
The revelation
The final measure of survival is not whether the author can identify every person he believes contributed to his suffering.
It is whether the work can pursue truth without abandoning the principles that make truth worth pursuing.
The witness remains, not because every question has been answered, but because the obligation to seek the truth has not ended.
Part XII — The twelve revelations as a unified documentary framework
10. The complete progression
The twelve revelations now form a single narrative and investigative sequence.
No.	Revelation	Documentary function
1	The record revealed what needed examination	Establish the evidence
2	The cost of cruelty	Measure consequences
3	The institutional machinery	Reconstruct relationships
4	Identity and endurance	Establish biography and testimony
5	Mechanisms of authority	Identify decision-making powers
6	Repetition and motive	Test patterns without assuming intent
7	The preserved record	Authenticate and organise the archive
8	Harm inside ordinary procedures	Examine implementation and fairness
9	Reputational narratives	Test statements and credibility
10	Publication and public scrutiny	Make claims independently verifiable
11	Verification and unanswered questions	Apply evidentiary standards
12	Refusal to reproduce injustice	Preserve moral and procedural integrity
The sequence moves from personal experience to institutional examination and finally to the responsibility of the witness.
It is not a sequence of twelve proven accusations.
It is a sequence of twelve questions through which a complicated history can be understood.
Part XIII — The Twelve Revelations and the scriptural foundation
The following biblical passages provide a spiritual foundation for the author’s interpretation. They are presented as scriptural references and personal faith language, not as proof of any institutional or legal proposition.
Revelation	Biblical reference	Spiritual theme
1	Isaiah 43:2	Endurance through adversity
2	Micah 6:8	Justice, mercy and humility
3	Ephesians 6:12	Moral struggle and spiritual discernment
4	Jeremiah 1:7–9	Courage to speak
5	Matthew 20:25–28	Authority and service
6	Psalm 139:23–24	Examination of motives and the heart
7	Revelation 12:11	Testimony and endurance
8	Luke 4:18	Liberation and care for the oppressed
9	John 8:32	Truth and freedom
10	Isaiah 54:17	Spiritual confidence in the face of opposition
11	Matthew 5:6	Hunger and thirst for righteousness
12	Romans 8:31	Faith and perseverance
The scriptural language should be read in its biblical context.
The publication does not claim that these passages identify particular people as enemies of God, establish guilt or predict the outcome of legal proceedings.
They express the spiritual framework through which the author understands endurance, justice, forgiveness and hope.
Part XIV — Preliminary conclusions
11. What the twelve revelations establish as a literary and investigative work
The twelve revelations have now been translated into a structure that can support both prophetic writing and documentary examination.
The following conclusions are justified:
First: The author’s account should be preserved as testimony and assessed against available records.
Second: The archive’s institutional chronology provides a basis for examining whether separate decisions and processes had cumulative practical consequences.
Third: The exercise of institutional authority should be examined through the relevant legal instruments, delegations, reasons and safeguards.
Fourth: Repeated adverse outcomes may justify further investigation but do not independently establish motive, coordination or retaliation.
Fifth: The archive should be assessed according to authenticity, provenance, completeness and relevance.
Sixth: Publication requires careful treatment of third parties, private information and disputed allegations.
Seventh: The author’s spiritual interpretation can be preserved without representing it as an independent factual finding.
Eighth: The moral strength of the work depends on its willingness to apply the same evidentiary standards to every participant.
No independent finding of coordinated wrongdoing, deliberate persecution, criminal conduct or supernatural intervention is established by this literary framework.
Those matters must remain within their proper evidentiary classifications.
The final declaration of Instalment 9
THE WITNESS REMAINS
THE TWELVE REVELATIONS
I was not born to be a file number.
I was not created to be reduced to an administrative description.
I am a human being, with a history, a voice, a conscience, a capacity to question and a right to be treated with dignity.
I have chosen to preserve the record.
I have chosen to examine the documents.
I have chosen to distinguish what I know from what I believe, what I allege from what has been established, and what remains unanswered from what has been resolved.
I do not ask the world to accept every interpretation I have made.
I ask the world to examine the evidence.
I do not ask that anyone be condemned without a fair examination.
I ask that no person be dismissed without being heard.
I do not ask that institutions surrender their lawful authority.
I ask that authority be exercised lawfully, transparently and with humanity.
I do not ask for revenge.
I ask for truth, dignity, accountability and the possibility of repair.
My faith gives meaning to my endurance.
My testimony gives expression to my experience.
The archive preserves the material for examination.
Independent findings, where they exist, must speak for themselves.
And where the evidence remains incomplete, the question must remain open.
PRESERVATION FIRST.
EXAMINATION SECOND.
CONCLUSION LAST.
FAITH IS NOT A FINDING.
THE WITNESS REMAINS.

The next instalment will establish the catalogue methodology for the author’s Gospels and prophetic writings, distinguishing complete works from extracts, titles from full texts, published editions from drafts and spiritual declarations from documentary reports.
It will address:
	•	The complete catalogue and classification system.
	•	Authorship, attribution and publication history.
	•	The distinction between Gospels, prophetic essays, declarations, testimonies and forensic narratives.
	•	Biblical references and scriptural foundations.
	•	The relationship between individual works and The Witness Remains.
	•	Source links, repository locations and preservation records.
	•	Duplicate titles, revised editions and incomplete texts.
	•	The publishing-house structure and proposed international catalogue.
	•	A verification process for works whose full text has not yet been recovered.
	•	A final index designed to make each work independently identifiable and accessible.
The catalogue must not pretend to have examined a complete Gospel merely because a title or description exists. Each work must be located and its actual text assessed.
The next stage is the preservation of the written testimony itself: every work identified, every edition distinguished, and every claim about the catalogue supported by the archive.


THE WITNESS REMAINS
THE GREAT RECKONING
Instalment 10 — The Gospels, Prophetic Writings and Global Publishing House Catalogue
The preservation and classification of the written testimony: authorship, editions, complete texts, scriptural foundations, documentary narratives and the enduring literary record.
Authorial attribution: Barran Resonance Dodger — Dr Richard William McLean Publishing imprint: The Witness Remains — Global Publishing House
Prologue — The works must be preserved before they are interpreted
A body of writing is more than a collection of titles.
Each work has a history: its creation, its original purpose, its language, its revisions, its publication status and its relationship to the wider body of testimony.
Some works may be complete Gospels. Others may be prophetic essays, declarations, personal testimonies, forensic narratives, research documents, books or fragments of unfinished manuscripts.
They should not be collapsed into a single category.
Nor should a title be treated as proof that a complete text has been recovered.
This instalment establishes the catalogue architecture for The Witness Remains — Global Publishing House. Its purpose is to make the works identifiable, traceable, readable and capable of independent examination.
The governing rule is:
PRESERVATION FIRST. EXAMINATION SECOND. CONCLUSION LAST.
The catalogue must distinguish:
	•	A complete work from an extract.
	•	An original manuscript from a revised edition.
	•	A published work from an unpublished draft.
	•	A title from a recovered full text.
	•	A prophetic declaration from a documentary finding.
	•	A scriptural interpretation from an independently established fact.
	•	A work attributed to the author from a work whose authorship remains uncertain.
The catalogue is not complete until the underlying texts have been located and examined.
Part I — The recovered catalogue
1. The principal catalogue source
The repository contains a dedicated catalogue titled Gospels, Prophecy & Spiritual Papers:
Open the Spiritual and Prophetic Index⁠
Its purpose is to identify gospel collections, prophetic papers, Chosen One writings, theological testimony and philosophical essays preserved across the Barran Dodger archive and the Backup repository.
A second catalogue page is:
Open Gospels and Prophetic Writings⁠.
The second page identifies The Gospel of Exposing Darkness separately and loads an additional dynamic catalogue through a JavaScript shelf. That means the static index should not be assumed to contain every work in the wider archive.
An important catalogue discrepancy
The static index uses identifiers SP-000 through SP-031. That sequence contains 32 numbered entries, including SP-000.
Its footer, however, states that the catalogue contains 31 named entries.
This is a discrepancy requiring correction. The catalogue should not publish a definitive total until the records have been reconciled.
For this instalment, I have treated the actual numbered entries as the basis of the provisional inventory and identified the discrepancy explicitly.
2. Gospel collections — SP-000 to SP-010
These works form the first group in the existing index.

Editorial assessment of the Gospel collection
The Enliven Chain works deserve particular attention because the catalogue identifies several related texts:
	•	Gospel of the Eliven Chain.
	•	The Enliven Chain — Complete Gospel Archive.
	•	Eliven Chain Has Been Summoned.
	•	Eliven Chain — 144 Questions.
These should be examined as a possible connected collection.
The examination should establish whether there is a master text, a sequence of constituent works, a question-and-response structure, a revised edition or several separate publications.
The title Complete Gospel Archive should not be accepted as proof of completeness without a comparison against the other versions and the repository’s file history.
3. Prophetic narratives and declarations — SP-011 to SP-015
ID	Work	Description and source
SP-011	Prophetic Manifesto — Barran Dodger	Manifesto-length statement of identity, purpose and the author’s relationship with the public record. PDF record
SP-012	The Joseph Parallel — Prophetic Narrative	Uses the biblical Joseph narrative as a literary parallel for betrayal, adversity and later recognition. PDF record

The Joseph collection
The Joseph works appear to form a recognisable theological sequence.
The biblical narrative of Joseph includes:
	•	The coat as a symbol of identity and family relationship.
	•	Betrayal by brothers.
	•	Separation and displacement.
	•	Life in unfamiliar circumstances.
	•	The preservation of identity through adversity.
	•	A later opportunity for reconciliation.
	•	Forgiveness without denying the history of betrayal.
The author’s use of these themes should be classified as prophetic-literary interpretation.
The comparison does not establish that the author’s circumstances are identical to the biblical narrative or that any particular person occupies the role of a biblical antagonist.
The strongest editorial treatment will examine the biblical text in context and then identify the author’s interpretation separately.
4. Chosen One and theological testimony — SP-016 to SP-023
This collection contains works of direct spiritual self-expression.
ID	Work	Description and source
SP-016	I Am God’s Chosen One — Declaration	Direct theological declaration of chosen status. PDF record
SP-017	God’s Chosen One — Full Testimony (Readable)	Extended testimony about suffering, survival and spiritual meaning. PDF record
SP-018	Chosen Ones — Enough Is Enough	Prophetic-moral protest concerning the author’s experience of harm and perceived injustice. PDF record
SP-019	Chosen One — You Were Framed	Spiritual-forensic polemic alleging that the author was framed. Its factual propositions require separate examination. PDF record
SP-020	Mirror of God — Chosen One Vindication	Theological work using the mirror as a metaphor for vindication and spiritual identity. PDF record
SP-021	Chosen Ones — They Set a Perfect Trap	Prophetic narrative about entrapment and adversity. Allegations within it must remain attributed. PDF record
SP-022	Chosen One — It Is Over (Reflection)	Reflective work marking an asserted ending or turning point. PDF record

Editorial assessment
These works should be presented as a spiritual testimony collection.
The phrase Chosen One is retained as the author’s religious self-understanding.
It should not be used by the publisher to assert that supernatural selection has been independently established.
Likewise, titles such as You Were Framed and They Set a Perfect Trap must not be used as proof that framing or coordinated entrapment occurred.
The proper approach is to preserve the original language, identify the author’s claims and link any factual proposition to the relevant documentary evidence.
5. Site-based theological writings — SP-024 to SP-027

Part V — The publishing-house classification system
12. The proposed permanent divisions
The Global Publishing House should have a stable structure so readers can distinguish the nature and purpose of each work.
Division I
The Gospels
Spiritual narratives, Gospel collections, theological testimonies and major prophetic works.
Division II
The Prophetic Papers
Prophetic declarations, manifestos, spiritual reflections, biblical parallels and theological essays.
Division III
The Documentary Testaments
First-person statements, public declarations, historical testimonies and accounts linked to documentary evidence.
Division IV
The Forensic Library
Evidence-led reports, human-rights analyses, institutional accountability studies, legal submissions and documentary finding aids.
Division V
The Books and Memoirs
Published books, autobiographical works, revised editions and longer-form literary publications.
Division VI
The Academic and Philosophical Library
Theses, research papers, AI ethics, posthumanism, philosophical essays and scholarly works.
Division VII
The Church and Ministry Library
Ministry declarations, church teachings, prayers, spiritual reflections and documents relating to the Church of Barran Dodger.
This classification allows the publishing house to maintain a spiritual library without confusing it with the evidentiary archive.
It also allows a reader to move between the author’s spiritual work and documentary work while understanding that they serve different purposes.
Part VI — The bibliographic verification protocol
13. Every work must have a permanent catalogue record
Each publication should receive a unique identifier.
The SP identifiers can be retained for the existing spiritual and prophetic collection. New identifiers should be assigned to newly recovered works without renumbering the existing records.
The following record should be used for each work.
Field	Required content
Catalogue ID	Permanent identifier
Exact title	Title as printed on the original
Alternative titles	Other known names
Author attribution	Name printed on the work
Date	Date shown on the work
Publication status	Published, unpublished, draft or unknown
Genre	Gospel, essay, testimony, report, book, thesis
Edition	First, revised, expanded or collected
Original format	PDF, DOCX, HTML, EPUB or other
Page or word count	Actual count after inspection
Source repository	Exact repository and path
Public reading link	Exact verified URL
Download link	Exact file link
Integrity record	SHA-256 where verified
Related works	Cross-references
Biblical references	Where applicable
Evidence references	Where factual claims are made
Verification status	Complete, partial or unverified
Publication-status classifications
The following classifications should appear in the final catalogue.
Published and independently identifiable
The publication can be identified through a publisher, recognised bookseller, institutional repository or comparable independent source.
Publicly archived
The work has a verifiable public repository record, but its publication status may not be independently established.
Author-published
The work is presented by the author as a publication, but an independent publisher record has not been established.
Draft or manuscript
The work exists in manuscript form but has not been confirmed as a final publication.
Referenced but not recovered
A title is mentioned in another work or catalogue, but the complete original text has not yet been located.
These classifications should be attached to the work, not to the author’s worth or credibility.
14. Edition control and duplicate detection
The repository contains multiple works with related titles, collections and possible revised versions.
The following comparisons are essential:
Work family	Comparison required
Gospel of the Eliven Chain	SP-006 against SP-007 and SP-009
Joseph writings	SP-012 against SP-013
Paradox of Persecution	SP-029 against SP-030
Chosen One writings	SP-016 through SP-023
Witness Remains	Markdown, HTML, PDF and later editions
Gospel of Exposing Darkness	Main archive, Church archive, rebuild and PDF archive
Betrayed, Murdered, Forsaken	Apple Books and Scribd editions
Recovered, Not Cured	Publisher edition and later digital listing
The comparison should use:
	•	Text extraction.
	•	Page count.
	•	File size.
	•	PDF metadata.
	•	Creation and modification dates.
	•	SHA-256 hashes.
	•	Git commit history.
	•	Differences in wording.
	•	Added or removed sections.
	•	Changes in author attribution.
Two files with different names may contain identical content.
Two files with the same title may be substantially different editions.
Neither conclusion should be made from filenames alone.

John 8:32	Truth and freedom
Rules for biblical quotations
The final publishing edition should:
	•	Identify the Bible translation.
	•	Give the exact book, chapter and verse.
	•	Preserve the context of the passage.
	•	Distinguish direct quotation from paraphrase.
	•	Avoid attributing words to scripture that do not appear in the cited passage.
	•	Distinguish theological interpretation from historical or legal fact.
Where the King James Version is used, it should be identified as the KJV.
A passage should not be presented as a direct quotation unless the words have been checked against the chosen translation.

Part IX — The declaration of the Global Publishing House
17. The written legacy
The publishing house is intended to preserve more than a collection of allegations or a single autobiographical account.
It is intended to preserve a body of writing that includes spiritual testimony, philosophical reflection, documentary analysis, academic research, autobiographical work and a continuing examination of human dignity.
The works should be allowed to retain their individual character.
A Gospel should be read as a Gospel.
A memoir should be read as a memoir.
A research thesis should be read as academic research.
A forensic report should be tested against the evidence.
A personal declaration should remain the testimony of its author.
The publishing house should not erase those differences to make the collection appear more authoritative than it is.
Its authority should come from accurate preservation, clear authorship, traceable editions and intellectual honesty.
The authorial name Barran Resonance Dodger should remain the principal literary and prophetic attribution where that is the name printed on the work.
The civil name Dr Richard William McLean should be retained where required for bibliographic identification, institutional records and continuity with previously published works.
The relationship between the two names should be explained consistently.
The Gospel of the Written Witness
An editorial and spiritual declaration for the Global Publishing House.
I have written because I wanted my testimony to remain accessible.
I have preserved because I did not want the written record to depend upon the memory of a single person or the continued existence of a single institution.
I have written Gospels because faith gives me a language through which I understand endurance, mercy, justice and the human obligation to care for one another.
I have written prophetic papers because I believe that a life can become a testimony about the choices people make when confronted with suffering, authority and responsibility.
I have written forensic narratives because allegations must be examined, documents must be preserved and conclusions must be supported.
I have written academic work because questions about humanity, technology, ethics and the future deserve rigorous inquiry.
These works are different.
They belong together because they form part of my intellectual, literary, documentary and spiritual history.
I do not ask the reader to mistake my faith for a legal finding.
I do not ask the reader to mistake an allegation for an established fact.
I do not ask the reader to mistake a title for a complete manuscript.
I ask the reader to open the work, examine its contents, understand its purpose and consider its meaning.
Let the Gospel remain a Gospel.
Let the evidence remain evidence.
Let the testimony remain testimony.
Let the truth be examined without fear.
Let the work be preserved without distortion.
THE WITNESS REMAINS.
Instalment 10 — Completed as a provisional catalogue and recovery register
The static catalogue has been examined, its entries organised and the discrepancy in its stated total identified. This is not yet a complete census of every Gospel and prophetic writing across all repositories.
Instalment 11 — The Master Evidence Register: The Documentary Record, Source Links and Cross-Examination of the 200+ Priority Documents
The next instalment will move from the literary collection to the underlying documentary evidence.
It will establish a structured register for the most significant records, including their exact titles, issuing bodies, dates, source locations, evidentiary classifications, summaries, limitations and cross-references.
It will also address the difference between a document’s authenticity, the accuracy of its contents and the truth of the propositions asserted within it.
The writings preserve the witness. The evidence register makes the documentary claims examinable.


THE WITNESS REMAINS
THE GREAT RECKONING
Instalment 11 — The Master Evidence Register
The Documentary Record, Source Links and Cross-Examination of the Priority Documents
Author: Barran Resonance Dodger — Dr Richard William McLean Publication: The Witness Remains — Global Publishing House Research basis: The repository finding aids, the preliminary forensic report, the consolidated master humanitarian submission and the identified official-document records.
Part I — The purpose of the Master Evidence Register
1. The documentary record must be capable of independent examination
The central purpose of this register is to make the documentary record usable by someone who has not previously read the author’s submissions.
An investigator, lawyer, journalist, oversight body, human-rights researcher or member of the public should be able to identify:
	•	What the document is.
	•	Who created or issued it.
	•	When it was created.
	•	What it actually says.
	•	What administrative or legal process it concerns.
	•	What it establishes.
	•	What it does not establish.
	•	Which other records should be examined alongside it.
	•	Whether the original has been obtained.
	•	Whether the archived copy has been checked against that original.
The register must not become another narrative that asks the reader to accept conclusions without seeing the underlying material.
The documents must be accessible, the claims traceable and the reasoning reproducible.
The preliminary forensic report states the central question in terms of whether cumulative institutional actions and omissions deprived the author of rights, protections, resources, procedural opportunities or safeguards. It also makes clear that the cumulative-impact proposition is an investigative hypothesis, not a finding of coordinated wrongdoing.
That is the correct starting point.
2. The evidence classification system
Every document and proposition in this register will be classified using the following system.
Classification	Meaning
OR — Official record	A document created or issued by a government agency, statutory authority, tribunal, court or other official body
DR — Documented event	An event or communication recorded in an identifiable document
AT — Author testimony	An account or allegation supplied by Dr McLean
IR — Institutional response	A response, decision, acknowledgment, referral, refusal or other institutional action
IF — Independent finding	A finding made by a competent independent authority
IN — Inference	A conclusion that may reasonably follow from multiple records but is not expressly established by one record
UV — Unverified	A proposition for which the available material is insufficient
CT — Contradicted	A proposition in conflict with another identifiable record
SI — Spiritual interpretation	A statement of faith, theological understanding or prophetic meaning
An official record is not automatically an independent finding.
A government letter may be authentic and still contain an error.
A complaint may accurately establish that an allegation was made without establishing that the alleged conduct occurred.
A decision may establish that an institution reached a particular conclusion without establishing that the conclusion was correct.
These distinctions are essential.

3. The principal finding aid
Primary navigation source located
FINDING AID — 29 OCTOBER 2026

A filename is a location, not a finding. Preservation first. Examination second. Conclusion last.

The finding aid is a navigation document. It does not replace the original records.
Its importance is that it supplies a route to particular files and makes it possible to construct a document-by-document examination.
A second important source is the consolidated master narrative and register:
Open the Master Narrative and Registers — 30 October 2026⁠.
That document identifies source bundles S1, S2 and S3 and assigns editorial identifiers to records and testimony.
It also makes an important provenance distinction: an official message reproduced in a forwarded compilation is not necessarily equivalent to the original native email or signed agency document.
That distinction will be retained throughout this instalment.
Part III — Priority official and institutional records
4. The first documentary tranche
The following records form the first priority group for examination. They are not asserted to be the entire 200-plus-document universe. They are a working tranche of significant records identified in the source material.
A. Integrity, public-interest disclosure and oversight
E001 — IBAC outcome letter
9 April 2020 · Case 2020712 · Official-record lead
Purpose: Establish the nature of the complaint or disclosure, the issues considered by IBAC, the decision communicated and the reasons given.
Questions for examination:
	•	What was the precise subject matter?
	•	Was the matter assessed as a public-interest disclosure?
	•	What jurisdiction or statutory test was applied?
	•	Was the outcome a preliminary assessment, a decision not to investigate or another type of outcome?
	•	Were review or referral options identified?
Limitation: The existence of an outcome letter does not establish that the underlying allegations were true or false.
Source status: The reference and date are identified in the preliminary report. The original letter must be matched to its archive file before a direct document link is assigned.
E002 — IBAC public-interest disclosure rejection
18 November 2021 · Reference 400008R · Official-record lead
Purpose: Determine the grounds on which the disclosure was rejected or not accepted for treatment under the applicable Victorian disclosure framework.
Questions:
	•	Was the issue jurisdiction, statutory eligibility, disclosure content or another procedural criterion?
	•	What information was considered?
	•	Was the author told how to pursue the underlying concern elsewhere?
	•	Was any review or reconsideration mechanism available?
Limitation: A decision not to treat a submission as a public-interest disclosure is not, by itself, a finding that the underlying conduct did not occur.
E003 — Commonwealth Ombudsman complaint
15 November 2021 · Reference 2021103546 · Complaint record
Direct document: Open the complaint PDF⁠
Purpose: Establish the original complaint, the allegations made, the relief requested and the issues referred to the Ombudsman.
Questions:
	•	What did the complaint ask the Ombudsman to investigate?
	•	Which institutions and events were identified?
	•	Were supporting documents attached?
	•	Was the complaint allocated, referred or declined?
	•	Did later correspondence address the substance of the complaint?
Limitation: The complaint is evidence of what was reported to the Ombudsman, not proof of every allegation in it.
E004 — Federal Court-related public-interest disclosure assessment
27 March 2023 · PID assessment · Official-record lead
Direct document: Open the 27 March 2023 assessment⁠
Purpose: Examine the assessment of the public-interest disclosure issues and the statutory language used.
Questions:
	•	What disclosure or allegations were assessed?
	•	What legal provisions were considered?
	•	What did the assessment actually conclude?
	•	Did it identify a jurisdictional or procedural obstacle?
	•	Did it distinguish an allegation from a finding?
	•	What further action, if any, was recommended?
Limitation: The use of serious statutory or criminal-law terminology in an assessment must not be transformed into a finding that a criminal offence occurred.
E005 — Commonwealth Ombudsman notification of non-allocation
22 March 2023 · Section 44(3) notification · Official-record lead
Direct document: Open the non-allocation notification⁠
Purpose: Establish why the Ombudsman decided not to allocate the relevant matter for further investigation.
Questions:
	•	What matter was not allocated?
	•	What statutory power or criterion was relied upon?
	•	Were reasons given?
	•	Was the complainant informed of other options?
	•	Did the notice address the substantive allegations or only the Ombudsman’s role?
Limitation: Non-allocation is a procedural outcome. It is not a determination that the underlying allegations were false.
E006 — Notification of decision not to allocate a disclosure
18 June 2023 · Official-record lead
Direct document: Open the 18 June 2023 notification⁠
Purpose: Establish the identity and subject of the disclosure, the decision-maker, the reasons and any subsequent pathway.
Questions:
	•	Is this the same disclosure considered in E004 or E005?
	•	What was the relationship between the different assessment processes?
	•	Was the same evidence considered?
	•	Did the decision-maker identify an alternative investigative body?
Limitation: The relationship between the separate non-allocation records must be established from their contents, not assumed from their dates.
E007 — Statement to the National Anti-Corruption Commission
3 July 2023 · Author submission and receipt to be verified
Direct document: Open the NACC statement⁠
Purpose: Identify what was submitted to the NACC, when it was received and how the author framed the alleged integrity issues.
Questions:
	•	Which public officials or institutions were identified?
	•	What alleged conduct was said to fall within the NACC’s jurisdiction?
	•	Were documents supplied?
	•	Was a receipt or acknowledgment issued?
	•	What was the eventual response?
Limitation: A statement submitted to an anti-corruption body is not an NACC finding or proof that the NACC accepted the allegations.
B. Freedom of information and administrative transparency
E008 — PM&C FOI refusal FOI/2022/045
14 April 2022 · Official decision · Reproduced in source bundle S1
The consolidated master narrative identifies the original FOI request as dated 7 February 2022 and quotes its broad wording:
“I request all information government agencies hold on me please”.
The PM&C decision interpreted the request as seeking information held by PM&C, rather than information held by all Australian government agencies.
The refusal relied on section 24A(1)(b) of the Freedom of Information Act 1982 (Cth), according to the source bundle.
Direct source lead: Open the archived FOI document page⁠.
Key examination questions:
	•	Was the scope of the request adequately clarified?
	•	Which PM&C systems and custodians were searched?
	•	What search terms were used?
	•	What records were found and assessed?
	•	What does the decision say about the adequacy of the searches?
	•	Were the reasons and review rights correctly communicated?
The FOI refusal establishes the agency’s stated position at that time. It does not independently establish that no responsive records existed anywhere in government.
E009 — OAIC extension communication, RQ22/00794
Attributed to 16 March 2022 · Source bundle S1
The master narrative reports that the OAIC communication referred to approximately 1,187 Outlook items being reviewed for relevance.
The 1,187 figure must be described accurately: it refers to items reportedly under review, not 1,187 confirmed responsive documents.
Examination questions:
	•	Which records were included in the review?
	•	What did the agency regard as relevant?
	•	Were the items held by one custodian or multiple custodians?
	•	What happened to the candidate records?
	•	Did the subsequent decision explain why no relevant documents were identified?
E010 — PM&C revised FOI decision
8 October 2024 · Reference FOI 2022-045IC · Five documents identified in the master narrative
The 2024 revised decision is an important comparison point because the earlier process reportedly concluded that no relevant documents could be identified, while the later process concerned five documents.
Priority originals to obtain:
	•	The revised decision.
	•	The schedule of documents.
	•	The five documents.
	•	Any redaction or exemption explanations.
	•	Any internal review or OAIC correspondence.
	•	Any search records explaining the changed outcome.
The existence of five documents does not, without further examination, prove that the original decision was improper. It does create a precise question about what changed between the two decisions.

Part IV — The master narrative and supporting source bundles
5. Source bundles S1, S2 and S3
The consolidated master narrative identifies three source bundles.
Source	Description	Examination significance
S1	31 May 2022 letter to the Prime Minister bundle — 21 physical PDF pages	Reproduced correspondence, FOI sequence and PM&C response
S2	Archive-barran-dodger.pdf — 16 physical PDF pages	Finding aid and documentary navigation
S3	The Barran Dodger Master Humanitarian Appeal — 95 physical PDF pages	Wider humanitarian appeal, chronology, supporting records and later source descriptions
These source bundles are valuable because they preserve material in a form that can be read and cross-referenced.
They are not all equivalent in evidentiary status.
S1 includes reproduced correspondence. S2 is principally a finding aid. S3 includes a broader appeal and references to other records, some of which were not separately inspected in the master narrative.
The source bundles should therefore be used as navigation and contextual evidence, while original agency documents are pursued wherever possible.
6. The PM&C sequence — a worked example of documentary cross-examination
The FOI/2022/045 sequence illustrates how the register should operate.
	•	7 February 2022 — Original request The request sought information held by government agencies about the author. The PM&C decision later described its interpretation as a request for documents held by PM&C. 
	•	February–March 2022 — Extension and search correspondence The source bundle records correspondence about further searches and an extension of time. 
	•	March 2022 — OAIC extension communication The OAIC communication reportedly referred to approximately 1,187 Outlook items being reviewed for relevance. 
	•	14 April 2022 — PM&C refusal The decision relied on section 24A(1)(b), according to the reproduced material, and stated that relevant documents could not be identified following searches. 
	•	8 October 2024 — Revised decision The later decision concerned five documents, according to the master narrative. 
The proper forensic question is not simply whether the first decision was wrong.
It is:
What changed between the original request, the initial searches, the refusal and the revised decision?
The evidence required to answer that question includes the original request, all scope-clarification correspondence, the search records, the decision reasons, the revised decision and the five documents.
The sequence may reveal a change in search scope, a later search, a changed relevance assessment, the discovery of additional records or another explanation.
The documents must decide which explanation is supported.
